# Story 27.3: Production Adapter and Deployment Profile

Status: in-progress

Execution gate: C0 passed on 2026-07-19 against Story 27.2's completed, reviewed handoff. The first approved 2026-07-20 course correction narrows this story to C1 qualification; the later approved 2026-07-20 on-premises correction replaces `PG-AZ-1` with `PG-ONPREM-1`. C1 blocks Production lifecycle writes and Story 27.4 until the exact Production profile passes every capability, security, durability, capacity, fault, backup/restore, and physical-reclamation gate. A41 remains open and is not mutable under Story 27.3.

**Narrowed 2026-07-28 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-28** (`_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/sprint-change-proposal-2026-07-28-c1-blocked-gate-split-and-c3-c4-ratification.md`), executing the Administrator decision of 2026-07-27. The thirteen C1 gate rows that carry no executable producer — C1.1-C1.12 and C1.14 — transfer to **Story 27.5**, keeping their identifiers. C1 completion for this story means the retained twelve rows (C1.13 and C1.15-C1.25). Production lifecycle writes remain disabled until the gates of both stories pass: this story reaching `done` does not enable them and does not advance Story 27.4. The same correction ratifies checkpoints C3 and C4, which are declared by the new AC7 and AC8.

<!-- Note: Validation is optional. Run validate-create-story for quality check before dev-story. -->

## Story

As a Platform Operations and security review pair,
I want one immutable Production state-store profile qualified against every C1 gate,
so that deployment-shaped lifecycle verification starts only on an atomic, durable, capacity-funded, and approved adapter.

## Acceptance Criteria

1. **Given** qualification target `PG-ONPREM-1`,
   **When** the running target is captured,
   **Then** its exact runtime, component, PostgreSQL 18.4 backend, Dapr control plane, application images, component/config manifests, actor/Scheduler identities, configuration epoch, profile hash, node/storage capacity, and operating cost are immutable evidence.

2. **Given** the exact captured profile,
   **When** the capacity result is computed for the 1-hour, configured 24-hour, and 7-day horizons,
   **Then** every operand is normalized to integer bytes/counts, the arithmetic is checked, and the computed result is admitted against the approved 70/80/90% threshold table without skip. *Narrowed 2026-07-28 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-28: CRUD, strong reads, ETags, rollback-atomic multi-key transactions, TTL, actor reactivation, Placement/Scheduler/reminder recovery, request bounds, two-writer 500 events/s throughput, 150,000-record purge catch-up, isolation, encryption, and cohort-attributable physical reclamation transfer to Story 27.5 as gates C1.1-C1.12 and C1.14, keeping their gate identifiers. No gate is dropped, weakened, or made discharge-able by a unit lane.*

3. **Given** the declared backend fault,
   **When** the PostgreSQL container/process is forcibly lost and its StatefulSet pod is replaced while the single node and retained local volume remain healthy,
   **Then** every Dapr-acknowledged record remains present; node, local-volume, control-plane, and site loss are explicitly outside profile; and backup/restore evidence plus the resulting nonzero RPO/RTO are published without an HA claim or overstatement.

4. **Given** complete immutable C1 evidence,
   **When** approvals are recorded,
   **Then** Hexalith Platform Operations separately approves node/storage capacity, operating cost, operation, bounded fault, backup/restore, upgrade, rollback, and reclamation and acknowledges the absence of node/disk/site HA, while a security reviewer approves identity, secrets, TLS, network, authorization, encryption, privacy, and evidence integrity.

5. **Given** any missing digest, placeholder, profile drift, failed probe, missing approval, or unreserved capacity,
   **When** C1 evaluates the profile,
   **Then** Production writes remain disabled, Story 27.3 remains `in-progress`, Story 27.4 remains `backlog`, and A41 remains open. *Extended 2026-07-28 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-28: the same fail-closed rule binds the thirteen gates transferred to Story 27.5. Production writes remain disabled while any of them is unproven, and Story 27.3 reaching `done` neither enables them nor advances Story 27.4.*

6. **Given** the kind-based production-deployment-verification lane,
   **When** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs it against the reviewed source,
   **Then** the four release OCI archives are built, the production manifests are rendered and applied to a disposable cluster, `tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1` produces evidence, `tools/validate-production-deployment-evidence.ps1` accepts it, and the evidence artifact is uploaded, with any failed render, apply, health, or evidence-validation step failing the lane rather than being skipped. *Added 2026-07-27 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-27 (DW 27.3-CR15). This criterion is independent of AC1-AC5: it neither requires nor unblocks C1, and passing it advances no C1 gate.*

7. **Given** the `Hexalith.Memories.AccessTelemetry.Tests` unit lane and a fresh Release build of it from the reviewed source,
   **When** the lane executes under the **Checkpoint Execution Contract** below,
   **Then** it proves the `state.postgresql/v2` access-telemetry adapter contract — transactional record-plus-expiry-index write and delete, ETag and `FirstWrite` semantics, `Conflict`/`StaleIndex`/`VerificationFailed`/`AlreadyAbsent` classification, ordering parity, and bucket-identity matching — against the in-process fakes. *Added 2026-07-28 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-28, ratifying checkpoint C3. This criterion is independent of AC1-AC5: it proves the adapter code contract, never the running target; passing it advances no C1 gate, enables no Production lifecycle write, and does not satisfy C1.11, whose cross-tenant denial must be observed against the running profile.*

8. **Given** the `ProductionDeploymentArtifactsTests` lane and a fresh Release build of `Hexalith.Memories.Server.Tests` from the reviewed source,
   **When** the lane executes under its own class selector under the **Checkpoint Execution Contract** below,
   **Then** it statically binds the reviewed `PG-ONPREM-1` manifests — `connectionString` resolving only through `secretKeyRef`, `actorStateStore: "true"`, `skipVerify`/`tlsServerName`, the ordered first-match `pg_hba` rules, least-privilege init-SQL grants, the RBAC secret-reader Roles, the deny-default lifecycle ACL, and the connection-pool arithmetic against `max_connections` including `maxSurge`/`maxUnavailable`. *Added 2026-07-28 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-28, ratifying checkpoint C4. This criterion is independent of AC1-AC5: it proves the manifests say what they must, never that the running deployment behaves as they say; passing it advances no C1 gate and enables no Production lifecycle write.*

- [ ] Task 1 - Qualify, pin, approve, and capacity-gate the exact `PG-ONPREM-1` Production Dapr component profile (AC: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5)
  - **Subtask ownership narrowed 2026-07-28 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-28.** No subtask text below is rewritten. The fourth subtask (the complete ADR behavioral probe), the fifth subtask (multi-key transaction fault injection), and the two-writer-workload/purge-backlog half of the eighth subtask transfer to **Story 27.5** with gates C1.1-C1.12 and C1.14; the threshold-table half of the eighth subtask stays here with C1.13. The first, second, third, sixth, seventh, ninth, tenth and eleventh subtasks stay with Story 27.3. Transferred subtask work must not be claimed as Story 27.3 evidence, and Story 27.3 must not implement it.
  - [ ] Use only profile ID `postgresql-v2-dapr-1.18.1-postgresql-18.4-onprem-k8s1-openebs-local-retain-400g-v1`: Dapr `state.postgresql/v2`; the official PostgreSQL 18.4 Trixie image pinned to index digest `sha256:3a82e1f56c8f0f5616a11103ac3d47e632c3938698946a7ad26da0df1334744a` and `linux/amd64` manifest `sha256:d93de42662696f278fb34354b06fdaa90ad7ca3106d6f72fbd01d16da006d2cf`; one StatefulSet replica; 4 CPU/8 GiB requests and 8 CPU/16 GiB limits; one 400 GiB `openebs-hostpath-retain` PVC local to `node1`; ClusterIP-only TLS `verify-full`; and the dedicated `memories_access_telemetry` database/`access_telemetry` schema/runtime role. This is explicitly non-HA. Any substitution changes the profile hash and requires another approved course correction.
  - [ ] Before exercising PostgreSQL v2, replace the unsupported `QueryStateAsync`/`queryIndexes` expiry scan with deterministic explicit minute/shard bucket state committed through Dapr transactions. Prove strong reads/ETags, bounded traversal, ordering, two-writer collision behavior, stale-index cleanup, and cross-tenant denial without adding a PostgreSQL SDK or connection string to product code.
  - [ ] Record the exact Dapr runtime and component implementation identity from the running deployment, not from .NET package pins: runtime version, sidecar image digest, component type/API version/capabilities, Scheduler connections, actor types, enabled features, component manifest/profile hash, backend identity/version, configuration epoch, and any alpha opt-in.
  - [ ] Run the complete ADR behavioral probe against the exact candidate: strong CRUD/reads, ETags, record-plus-expiry-index atomicity, deterministic conflict behavior, actor state/reactivation/placement/reminders, effective TTL, request bounds, two-writer throughput during purge, durability, isolation/encryption, capacity, and physical-evidence hooks.
  - [ ] Fault-inject a later operation in the multi-key transaction and prove no partial record or expiry-index commit. Do not certify Redis from Dapr's capability table: the official Dapr 1.18 Redis implementation warns that transactions have no rollback and are unsuitable as a Production actor store unless the exact profile proves the ADR's atomicity contract.
  - [ ] Publish the adapter's declared single-component fault and prove zero acknowledged-record loss for that exact fault. A candidate that cannot prove zero is ineligible. Publish RPO/RTO only for failures outside the declared single-component profile, plus the physical-reclamation collector/bound, upgrade and rollback owner, and an approval by Hexalith Platform Operations plus the security reviewer.
  - [ ] Calculate `records × (measuredRecordBytes + measuredIndexBytes) × durabilityMultiplier + controlBytes + reclamationWorkspace` for 1 hour, the configured 24-hour target, and the 7-day software maximum. Normalize every operand to integer bytes/counts before arithmetic; reject missing units, fractional counts, negative values, `NaN`/infinity, values above signed 64-bit input bounds, overflow, and mixed decimal/binary units; use checked/arbitrary-precision arithmetic and round required capacity upward. Include actor/index state, WAL/snapshot overhead, allocator fragmentation, rewrite/vacuum workspace, shared-node headroom, Scheduler storage, operating cost, and decommission headroom. Admit only durations whose measured result fits the exact 400 GiB profile; reject a higher duration until a new capacity result/profile is approved. Treat the PVC request as non-reserving local storage and capture host filesystem headroom.
  - [ ] Enforce steady state <=70%, reclamation peak <=80%, critical at 80%, Unhealthy at 90%, and the exact ADR two-writer workload at 500 events/s while purge runs: per writer, 200 search, 6 ingest, 10 traverse, 16 case-access, 2 delete, 0.2 tenant-lifecycle, 0.8 tenant-config, 4 case-member, and 11 annotation events/s, using the ADR's deterministic 90/10 success/error fixture and bounded P95 payloads. Require a 30-minute steady-state window plus the 10-minute 150,000-due-record purge backlog, zero acknowledged loss/unclassified errors, p99 transaction latency below the configured 3-second Dapr client timeout, no more than 10% p95 latency regression from the same-profile no-purge baseline, and complete queue/backlog drain within five minutes with oldest-due age below 15 minutes. The committed single 20 GiB Redis StatefulSet cannot be presumed eligible: the ADR's 24-hour canonical payload is already 20.60 GiB before overhead.
  - [ ] Keep Production lifecycle writes disabled/fail-closed until this checkpoint passes. Memories and the lifecycle service remain Dapr-only; no backend SDK, connection string, backend credential, or orchestrator API enters application code.
  - [ ] Prove the declared pod/process-loss fault with zero acknowledged-record loss while `node1` and the retained volume remain healthy. Publish node, local-volume, control-plane, and site loss as outside profile, attach a named backup destination and successful restore evidence, publish the resulting nonzero RPO/RTO, and never claim node/disk/site HA.
  - [ ] If `PG-ONPREM-1` cannot pass every atomicity, zero-loss, configured-retention capacity, security, backup/restore, and physical-reclamation gate, preserve its rejection and exact failed command, keep Production writes disabled and A41 open, and run a new correct-course decision. Never weaken a gate or silently substitute another profile.

### Scope Transferred to Story 27.4

The approved 2026-07-20 course correction moves former Tasks 2-8 and checkpoints C2-C6 to Story 27.4: multi-writer/replacement/durability proof; expiry, purge, newer-record preservation, and cohort-attributable reclamation; failure/privacy/authority/health/metrics/alerts; lifecycle runbook and exact-adapter operations appendix; closure-owned residual reconciliation; evidence-backed A41 mutation; and terminal governed validation. None of that scope is executable under Story 27.3, and Story 27.4 remains `backlog` until this story is `done` with an immutable approved C1 packet.

### Scope Transferred to Story 27.5 (2026-07-28)

The approved 2026-07-28 course correction executes the Administrator decision of 2026-07-27 (eighth-invocation code review): the blocked C1 gate set moves to a newly numbered story.

**To Story 27.5 (Epic 27)** — the thirteen C1 gates that carry no executable producer while the `PG-ONPREM-1` lifecycle environment is disabled: **C1.1** CRUD, **C1.2** strong reads, **C1.3** ETags, **C1.4** rollback-atomic multi-key transactions, **C1.5** TTL, **C1.6** actor reactivation, **C1.7** Placement/Scheduler/reminder recovery, **C1.8** request bounds, **C1.9** two-writer 500 events/s throughput, **C1.10** 150,000-record purge catch-up, **C1.11** isolation, **C1.12** encryption, and **C1.14** cohort-attributable physical reclamation. Story 27.5 also owns authoring each row's operator-executed command against the running target — the work that has no owner today. The gates keep their existing identifiers so every prior citation in this ledger, in `deferred-work.md`, and in the committed evidence packets stays resolvable.

**Retained by Story 27.3** — C0; the twelve remaining C1 rows (**C1.13** capacity admission and **C1.15-C1.25** profile identity, capacity/cost, declared-fault durability, backup/restore, RPO/RTO, and the two separated approvals); and the three non-C1 lanes C2, C3 and C4.

**No file transfers.** The thirteen gates have no producer in the repository today, so no path implements them. `tools/verify_access_telemetry_lifecycle.py` states in its own docstring that it is not a behavioural prober and stays with Story 27.3 for C1.13 and C1.15-C1.25; the evidence packet and its per-run archive stay here too. Story 27.5 declares its own `## File Scope` when `create-story` authors it.

Transferred scope is not executable under Story 27.3 and may not be claimed as Story 27.3 evidence. Story 27.5 is `backlog` behind an activation gate owned by Hexalith Platform Operations: the `PG-ONPREM-1` lifecycle Deployments scaled above zero with the production flag and profile hash set and the clock authorities pointed at real endpoints. AC5's fail-closed rule binds both stories, so this story reaching `done` neither enables Production lifecycle writes nor advances Story 27.4.

### Scope Transferred to Stories 30.1 and 31.1 (2026-07-26)

The approved 2026-07-26 course correction executes the two splits the Administrator approved on 2026-07-21 during code review.

**To Story 30.1 (Epic 30, DW 27.3-CR5)** — the four-image container release and partial-recovery pipeline, with its CI wiring, tooling, tests, and the release-runbook four-image expansion.

**To Story 31.1 (Epic 31, DW 27.3-CR6)** — the OpenBao `hexalith-keys` platform and the runtime Dapr `secretstore` migration from Kubernetes Secrets to `hashicorp.vault` for the `eventstore` and `memories` scopes. Story 27.3 retains the access-telemetry-specific secret components and the `PG-ONPREM-1` secret backing, which remain in adapter scope.

**Environment prerequisite, not a story dependency:** the `PG-ONPREM-1` connection string, including `sslmode=verify-full`, resolves from the deployed OpenBao platform. That platform is already running — the 2026-07-20 live probe loaded the store — so Story 31.1 formalizes ownership and hardening of an operational platform rather than standing one up. Story 27.3's C1 probe is not blocked on Story 31.1. **Corrected 2026-07-26 by code review (chunk 3b), per the Administrator decision recorded below:** a forward dependency on Story 30.1 *does* exist and is accepted. The 27.3-retained `tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1` declares `-AccessTelemetryArchive` and `-AccessTelemetryClockArchive` as `[Parameter(Mandatory)]`, and both `.tar.gz` archives are produced only by the transferred `tools/publish-containers.ps1` (Story 30.1); the C1 reopen trigger recorded in the Debug Log additionally requires signed registry publication for all four images, which is Story 30.1 acceptance scope. Story 30.1 must therefore not regress this verification lane. The earlier blanket claim that "no forward dependency is created" was wrong and is superseded by this paragraph.

Neither transferred block is executable under Story 27.3, and neither may be claimed as Story 27.3 evidence. Both target stories are `backlog`.

**Owner update 2026-07-27 (approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-27, DW 27.3-CR16).** The two paragraphs above are the append-only record of the 2026-07-26 transfer and are not rewritten, but their target stories have since been split and their owners have moved. The Story 30.1 block is now Stories 30.1, 30.3, 30.4 and 30.5; in particular `tools/publish-containers.ps1` - the producer of the two `.tar.gz` archives this story's `tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1` declares `[Parameter(Mandatory)]` - is now **Story 30.3**-owned, so the accepted forward dependency recorded above is on Story 30.3, not Story 30.1. `tests/Hexalith.Memories.Cli.Tests/Ci/CiTestInventoryTests.cs` remains Story 30.1-owned. The Story 31.1 block is now Stories 31.1 and 31.2. All six target stories remain `backlog`.

- [x] [Review][Patch] `_normalize_bytes` accepts unit-less values as bytes, defeating Task 1's explicit reject-missing-units requirement - a bare `int` short-circuits at line 168 and a unit-less string defaults to `"B"` at line 176, so `"400"` meaning 400 GB is silently read as 400 bytes and `"8b"` as 8 bytes; the mixed-unit check then excludes `"none"`, so mixing `"1KiB"` with bare `1024` passes. Also harden: an empty `horizons` mapping silently substitutes the three defaults, zero measured record/index bytes yields a `required_bytes = 0` "proof", and operands above the 28-digit decimal context round to integral and bypass the fractional-rejection path. The fixture itself feeds bare ints and must be updated. Mutation-proven (restoring the bare-int acceptance failed 2 cases).

- [x] [Review][Patch] The in-memory adapter diverges from the adapter it substitutes for on four behaviours the lifecycle checkpoints depend on - a duplicate entry key throws `ArgumentException` *after* `_records` was mutated (a non-atomic write where the Dapr store returns `Conflict`), `VerificationFailed` is unreachable so the durability defect the enum exists for is never exercised, `ttlInSeconds` is accepted and discarded with no validation, and the due-scan has no catalog or empty-minute side effect so repeat scans diverge. [src/Hexalith.Memories.AccessTelemetry/Lifecycle/InMemoryAccessTelemetryStateStore.cs:82] — applied 2026-07-26, all four: a duplicate entry slot now returns `Conflict` decided **before** any mutation instead of throwing `ArgumentException` after `_records` was written; `VerificationFailed` is reachable through a `SuppressRecordDeletion` hook and the same strong post-delete verification the Dapr adapter performs; `ttlInSeconds` is validated (non-positive raises) and observable via `LastTtlInSeconds`; and the due scan is minute-major over an active-minute catalog that prunes drained minutes and leaves untraversed minutes alone. - [x] [Review][Patch] The deployment-guard helpers are structurally unsound, so several guards assert less than they claim - `ReadComponentMetadata` takes the next `value:` anywhere after the name with no item bound, returning a neighbour's value for any `secretKeyRef`-backed key; `ReadYamlSequence` runs to end-of-document when the terminator is absent and inspects only the first key occurrence; `max_connections` and `reserved_connections` are searched only in the StatefulSet document and only at the first occurrence, so setting either through `access-telemetry-postgresql-config` voids the check; the pg_hba assertions are presence-only and ignore first-match-wins ordering, so a bare `host ... scram-sha-256` line inserted before the reject rules accepts plaintext TCP while all four assertions pass; and the ACL guards are case-sensitive substring counts, so `httpVerb: ["delete"]` bypasses `ShouldNotContain("DELETE")`. [tests/Hexalith.Memories.Server.Tests/Deployment/ProductionDeploymentArtifactsTests.cs:442] — applied 2026-07-26, all five: `ReadComponentMetadata` binds to its own `- name:` item and requires exactly one inline value (with a separate `ReadComponentMetadataSecretRef` for `secretKeyRef`-backed entries); `ReadYamlSequence` binds by indentation, requires the key to occur exactly once, and handles a key that is itself a sequence item; `max_connections`/`reserved_connections` are searched across the whole rendered manifest and must occur exactly once; the pg_hba rules are compared as an exact ordered list so a `host` rule inserted above the reject rules fails; and the ACL verb exclusions are case-insensitive. Mutation-proven: a plaintext-capable `host` rule above the rejects, a lowercase `delete` verb, and a repointed `connectionString` secret each fail the class.

[tests/Hexalith.Memories.Server.Tests/Deployment/ProductionDeploymentArtifactsTests.cs:326] — applied 2026-07-26: the guard reads `spec.replicas` and `maxSurge` out of the rendered overlay and computes peak concurrent pods as `max(declared, C1 probe) + maxSurge`. Mutation-proven by setting `maxSurge: 1`.

- [x] [Review][Decision] Which capacity admission threshold governs — the story (`:60`) admits durations "whose measured result fits the exact 400 GiB profile" (429,496,729,600 bytes, 100%), while the approved correction admits only results fitting its thresholds (70% steady state = 300,647,710,720). A measured 24-hour result between those two values is admitted by one authority and rejected by the other, with no precedence rule. Two adjacent gaps need the same decision: 80% is simultaneously "reclamation peak <=80%" and "critical at 80%" (`:61`, proposal `:142`), and no rule states the consequence when the *configured* 24-hour target itself fails capacity — "reject a higher duration" does not apply to the configured duration, and the 1-hour horizon has no stated consequence at all. - **Decided 2026-07-26 by Administrator during code review:** the approved correction's 70/80/90% threshold table governs. Amend the story's "fits the exact 400 GiB profile" wording to cite that table as the admission gate, resolve the 80% peak-vs-critical collision, and define the disposition when the configured 24-hour target itself fails capacity. Routed to patch. — resolved 2026-07-26 during this review.

**Chunk-1 review note (2026-07-27).** The diff `c9dfb06f..HEAD` (work since the chunk-3b review) is reviewed in three chunks: Chunk 1 (this pass) covers the ten `AccessTelemetry` Lifecycle adapter source/test paths; Chunk 2 (production-deployment verification tooling) and Chunk 3 (governance/planning docs, including this story file's own sections) are not yet reviewed. No status transition is valid until all three chunks report complete, per `_bmad/custom/story-phase-ledger.md`'s chunked-review rule. Six layers ran (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Verification Gap, Acceptance Auditor, Historical Slice Guard, Story Phase Ledger Auditor); the Acceptance Auditor independently rebuilt and ran the four affected test assemblies (AccessTelemetry.Tests 85/85, Cli.Tests `CiTestInventoryTests` 59/59, Server.Tests `AccessTelemetryRetentionDecisionTests` 10/10, IntegrationTests `AccessTelemetryLifecycleIntegrationCheckpointTests` 8/8) and found no acceptance-criteria violation. 15 raised findings were dismissed after independently reading the actual source (one — a claim that `TransactionalDaprState.Apply` validates and mutates in the same loop — was verified false against the code; the rest were confirmed real but unreachable via the sole production call path, already covered by an existing deferred item, or consistent with an established codebase-wide pattern); see Debug Log References for the full list.

**Chunk-1 resolution (2026-07-27).** All six chunk-1 items (one `[Review][Decision]`, five `[Review][Patch]`) are now applied and checked off. Commits `1f83c4ab` and `000af156` landed independently of this review session while the decision above was being resolved and implemented the decision plus four of the five patches (verified: re-read the actual diff against each finding, then independently rebuilt and ran `Hexalith.Memories.AccessTelemetry.Tests` fresh — `88/88` — and `CiTestInventoryTests` fresh — `59/59`). This code-review phase applied the remaining patch (the C1.11 citation) directly, per the routing its own finding required. Chunk 1 is closed; Chunks 2 and 3 remain, now against HEAD `000af156` (which also touched this story file, `deferred-work.md`, and evidence artifacts outside chunk 1's scope — those fall to Chunk 3).

- [x] [Review][Decision] `InMemoryAccessTelemetryStateStore`'s `StaleIndex` delete branch has no post-removal strong verification (unlike its own main `Deleted`/`AlreadyAbsent` branch and unlike `DaprAccessTelemetryStateStore.RemoveBucketEntryAsync`, which re-reads and throws `InvalidOperationException` on failure); separately, even the main branch's existing check can never trigger because this fake has no hook to force an "acknowledged-but-unapplied" state, unlike `TransactionalDaprState`'s `UndeletableKeys`/`UnappliedUpsertKeys`. The class's own doc comment claims to model "a strong post-delete verification that can fail" for parity with the Dapr adapter, but neither branch of this fake can actually fail that verification, so the "portable" checkpoint path can never exercise `VerificationFailed` — the same failure mode is fully covered via the Dapr-fake-based tests instead. Decision needed: invest in closing this test-fidelity gap (add force-failure hooks plus a `StaleIndex`-side check and dedicated tests, mirroring `TransactionalDaprState`), or accept the asymmetry given the class is never registered with the runtime host and the failure mode is already proven elsewhere? [src/Hexalith.Memories.AccessTelemetry/Lifecycle/InMemoryAccessTelemetryStateStore.cs:248] — **applied 2026-07-27 per the Administrator decision (close it, and correct the finding).** The finding's second claim was false when raised and is corrected here: `VerificationFailed` *was* already reachable on the main delete branch through the `SuppressRecordDeletion` hook, and `DeleteAndVerifyAsync_BackendKeepsRecordAfterAcknowledgedDelete_ReturnsVerificationFailed` exercised it (passing). The genuine gap was narrower — only the `StaleIndex` branch pruned its entry with no post-removal verification. Closed by mirroring `DaprAccessTelemetryStateStore.RemoveBucketEntryAsync`: a `SuppressEntryRemoval` hook plus a `Seed` helper make the acknowledged-but-unapplied prune constructible, the `StaleIndex` branch now strongly re-reads and throws `InvalidOperationException` when the entry survived, and the prune is guarded on presence so it fires only when the entry was actually held — exactly the Dapr adapter's `if (bucketContainsEntry)` condition. Pinned by the new `DeleteAndVerifyAsync_StalePruneIsAcknowledgedButNotApplied_ThrowsRatherThanReportingStaleIndex`, RED-proven: with the verification removed the class runs `88` with exactly `1` failure — that test — and all 87 pre-existing tests stay green.

Update the citation so a reviewer following it into the codebase finds the actual test [_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/27-3-production-adapter-and-deployment-profile.md:396] — applied 2026-07-27 by this code-review phase (the routing this finding required per the 2026-07-27 fifth dev-story invocation's Debug Log note: the C1 Gate Evidence Table sits under Implementation Checkpoints, not a `dev-story`-permitted section, so only the owning `code-review` phase repairs it). The row now cites `...IsDeniedAndLeavesTheRecordPurgeable`, matching live source.
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Medium] `CiWorkflow_KeepsEveryRestoreAuditExceptionInSyncWithDirectoryBuildProps` counts only `dotnet restore` invocations that already carry `-p:WarningsNotAsErrors=`; a new restore step added without that flag at all isn't matched, so the count stays at `4` and the guard stays green on exactly the drift its own comment says it exists to catch [tests/Hexalith.Memories.Cli.Tests/Ci/CiTestInventoryTests.cs:427] — applied 2026-07-27: the guard now matches **every** `dotnet restore` invocation (`dotnet restore [^\r\n]*`), requires that total to be exactly `4`, and separately requires every one of them to carry `-p:WarningsNotAsErrors=` before comparing the injected codes. Proven on the finding's own scenario: with a fifth restore step added carrying no flag at all, the **pre-fix** guard passes green (`Total: 1, Failed: 0`) while the fixed guard fails. A second mutation — stripping the flag from one of the four existing steps, keeping the count at `4` — fails the new per-step assertion with its own message.
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Low] `CiWorkflow_RunsEveryToolingFixtureSuiteThatGuardsAShippedTool` has no `.ShouldNotBeEmpty()` guard before its `foreach` over `tests/tooling` subdirectories, so the test would vacuously pass with zero assertions if that directory ever had none; separately its literal `-s tests/tooling/{suite} ` substring match (trailing space) is brittle to harmless workflow-YAML reformatting, though it fails closed (loud failure) rather than silently [tests/Hexalith.Memories.Cli.Tests/Ci/CiTestInventoryTests.cs:460] — applied 2026-07-27: the suite list is materialized and asserted `ShouldNotBeEmpty` before the `foreach`, so the vacuous-pass path is closed. The literal `-s tests/tooling/{suite} ` substring is replaced by `-s\s+tests/tooling/{escaped}(?![0-9A-Za-z_-])`, which tolerates whitespace reformatting while still binding the path as a whole token. Mutation-proven: re-pointing one wired suite to `access_telemetry_lifecycle_typo` fails the guard with `tests/tooling/access_telemetry_lifecycle is executed by no workflow step`, confirming the token boundary rejects a longer name as satisfying the real suite.
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Low] No explicit test pins the `partitionKey`/`ttlInSeconds` metadata contract on delete-path transaction operations — only `WriteRecordAndIndexAsync_CarriesPartitionAndTtlMetadataTheComponentRequires` asserts it, on the write path. The existing green suite implicitly proves the delete path is correct too (`RequireMetadata` throws unconditionally for any operation with wrong metadata), but there is no dedicated documentation-grade assertion for it [tests/Hexalith.Memories.AccessTelemetry.Tests/Lifecycle/DaprAccessTelemetryStateStoreTests.cs:1] — applied 2026-07-27: `DeleteAndVerifyAsync_CarriesPartitionMetadataAndNeverTtlOnAnyOperation` asserts, on the delete transaction specifically, that every operation carries `partitionKey: access-telemetry`, that none carries `ttlInSeconds` (only records expire natively), and that the transaction contains a `Delete`. All six layers ran again fresh. The Acceptance Auditor independently ran the runnable lanes this diff touches (13/13 `access_telemetry_lifecycle`, 27/27 `production_deployment_evidence`, 31/31 `coverage_gate`, 42/42 `story_scope`, 7/7 `integration_stub_closure`) and cross-checked several claims against a live `kubectl kustomize` render of the production overlay; it also disproved a plausible-looking hypothesis that this diff's OpenBao-variable cleanup caused the currently-red `production-deployment-verification` CI run (run `30246564974`, `DW 27.3-CR17`) — that code was already dead/vestigial before this diff, and the real root cause (`deploy/kubernetes/base/dapr/secretstore.yaml` requiring OpenBao, which the disposable `kind` cluster never gets) predates this whole reviewed range. 20 raised findings were dismissed after independently reading the actual source and, in several cases, executing the code in question (`DECLARED_SINGLE_COMPONENT_FAULT` empty/whitespace bypass — false, `EnvironmentIdentity.from_mapping` already strips and rejects empty required fields before the placeholder-set check ever runs; `canonical_pg_onprem_profile()`'s dependency on the overridable `REVIEWED_KUBE_CONTEXT` — intentional, matches the already-applied 2026-07-26 fix, "fixing" it would regress that fix; the case-insensitive `DELETE`/`PUT`/`PATCH` ACL check — deliberate and documented, narrowly scoped to one Dapr `Configuration` document; a rolling-update outage risk from `maxSurge:0`/`maxUnavailable:1` — moot today, `replicas: 0`; a commented-out `#max_connections` collision and a "CI step silently passes with 0 tests" claim — both verified absent/false against the real manifest and `unittest discover`'s actual exit-5-on-zero-match behavior; plus ~11 minor test/tooling nitpicks consistent with this repo's existing risk tolerance elsewhere). See Debug Log References for the full list.

**Chunk-2 resolution (2026-07-27).** Both decisions and all 6 patches (8 items total) are applied and checked off. The health-evidence fix (scope the succeeded-run body/status contract to each stage's last attempt only) and the fallback-timeout shortening were implemented directly, each backed by a genuine RED-then-GREEN test (`test_succeeded_run_tolerates_a_malformed_earlier_attempt_for_the_same_stage` / `test_succeeded_run_fails_when_the_terminal_attempt_itself_is_malformed`). Verified: `production_deployment_evidence` 30/30, `access_telemetry_lifecycle` 14/14, `coverage_gate` 31/31, `story_scope` 42/42, all fresh. The C# `ProductionDeploymentArtifactsTests.cs` changes (a `maxUnavailable` assertion, `ReadYamlSequence` comment tolerance, `ReadHostBasedAuthenticationRules` tab-normalization consistency) could not be built/run in this sandbox (pre-existing `NU1102` on `Hexalith.EventStore.Client`, the same limitation the Acceptance Auditor hit reviewing this chunk) — verified instead by tracing the logic against a fresh `kubectl kustomize` render of the production overlay, confirming no tabs or comment lines exist in current content so both changes are strictly additive. Chunk 2 is closed; Chunk 3 (governance/planning docs) remains.

- [x] [Review][Patch] The new per-attempt health-evidence persistence can make a genuinely successful rollout fail evidence validation. `Save-HealthResponseEvidence` now writes one `health-$stage-$attempt.json` per poll instead of overwriting the previous attempt, and `Wait-AggregateStatus` calls it every ~2 seconds inside its polling loop — including during the `-MeasureFromContainerRunning` window that exists specifically to cover the gap between "container Running" and "app accepting real connections" (`initial-server-health`/`initial-mcp-health` stages). `validate-production-deployment-evidence.ps1`'s unchanged succeeded-branch check requires *every* `health-*.json` file to have a parsable JSON body and a status in `(200, 503)` when `verification-result.json` reports `succeeded`. So a single early poll during that startup gap — expected to return a non-JSON transcript or a `$null` status, which is exactly what the gap-measurement exists to tolerate — now survives as its own file and fails the whole evidence-validation step, even though the deployment run legitimately succeeded. Before this diff, that same early-poll file was silently overwritten by the next, successful poll, so only the terminal state was ever validated. **Decision (2026-07-27, Administrator, during this code review): scope the succeeded-branch's strict body/status check to each stage's *last* attempt only** — every attempt stays retained as evidence, but only the terminal attempt per stage must be well-formed JSON with a 200/503 status when the run succeeded. [tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1:261] [tools/validate-production-deployment-evidence.ps1:73] — applied 2026-07-27: the validator now groups health records by stage, tracks each stage's highest `attempt`, and applies the succeeded-run body/status check only to that terminal record; non-terminal attempts are skipped. Proven both directions: `test_succeeded_run_tolerates_a_malformed_earlier_attempt_for_the_same_stage` (an early malformed attempt no longer fails a succeeded run) and `test_succeeded_run_fails_when_the_terminal_attempt_itself_is_malformed` (the terminal attempt is still held to the contract) both pass.
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Medium] `verify-production-deployment.ps1`'s netcat fallback now fires on *any* non-zero `wget` exit instead of only when the output contains a `503` line — a real, reasoned fix (the fallback previously missed exactly the slow-start/timeout cases it exists for, per its own updated comment). The cost: every failed poll now costs up to ~11s (5s sleep + 6s `nc` timeout) instead of returning near-immediately. Polls run every ~2 seconds inside `Wait-AggregateStatus`'s `-MeasureFromContainerRunning` loop, and several consecutive early-startup polls typically fail before the app is ready — so as few as 3 slow failed polls (about 57s) can now approach or exceed the stage's own 60-second startup budget, purely from added latency, independent of whether the app itself is starting any slower than before. **Decision (2026-07-27, Administrator, during this code review): shorten the fallback's own timeouts** rather than raising the stage timeout, to keep the per-failed-poll cost low without weakening the 60-second startup budget itself. [tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1:230] — applied 2026-07-27: `sleep 5`/`nc -w 6` reduced to `sleep 2`/`nc -w 3`, cutting the worst-case per-failed-poll cost from ~11s to ~5s; `production_deployment_evidence_test.py`'s pinned `nc -w 6` assertion updated to `nc -w 3` to match.

The unlabeled high-entropy-secret detection in `_redact` was left as accepted residual risk — a robust version is a real feature (risking over-redaction of legitimate hashes/IDs), not a narrow patch, and this only affects the richness of a human-reviewed diagnostic packet.

- [x] [Review][Decision] [High] The umbrella exemption's own precondition was removed by this diff — `story-scope-guard.md:32-34` and `epics.md:555` permit Story 27.3 to remain one tracking story only while every checkpoint carries an owner, validation command/artifact, review status and completion date. This diff replaces the command cell of 13 of the 25 C1 gate rows with `Required observation, via a command not yet authorable`, and the same diff hardens `tools/verify_access_telemetry_lifecycle.py` to disclaim being their producer. The judgement not to author 13 unrunnable commands is right; the consequence is that the exemption's stated basis no longer holds and no compensating split or approved sprint change was recorded. Options: (a) split the environment-blocked C1 gate set into a newly numbered story; (b) obtain an approved sprint change restating the exemption on a different basis. Do not resolve by re-attaching the withdrawn citations. **RESOLVED 2026-07-27 by Administrator: split the blocked C1 gate set into a newly numbered story.** Execution requires `correct-course`; it is outside this code-review's mandate. Story 27.3 stays `in-progress` until that split lands.
- [x] [Review][Decision] [High] Checkpoints C3 and C4 were created and marked `complete` with no approved sprint change and no `epics.md` amendment — verified absent from every file in `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/`, whereas the immediately preceding C2/AC6 carries `sprint-change-proposal-2026-07-27-profile-hash-deployment-ac-and-epic-splits.md` and an `epics.md` entry. The story's own precedent is that adding a checkpoint to 27.3 requires that instrument. Options: (a) ratify C3/C4 through correct-course plus the matching `epics.md` amendment; (b) spin the PG-v2 adapter lane and the manifest-guard lane out as newly numbered stories; (c) reopen C3/C4 to `pending`. **RESOLVED 2026-07-27 by Administrator: ratify C3/C4 through correct-course plus the matching `epics.md` amendment**, matching the C2/AC6 precedent. Execution requires `correct-course`; C3/C4 remain unratified until it lands.
- [x] [Review][Decision] [High] C3 and C4 completion evidence fails the Checkpoint Execution Contract they explicitly cite — the contract (line 412) requires that "every selector discovers at least one unique method, `Total` equals the selected discovery count and is greater than zero, and `Errors`, `Failed`, `Skipped`, and `Not Run` are all zero", and states that a missing selector or count mismatch "fails the checkpoint even when the process exit code is zero". C4's command carries `-class Hexalith.Memories.Server.Tests.Deployment.ProductionDeploymentArtifactsTests` but its evidence cites a full-assembly run of `2,745` cases with `Skipped = 1` and no selector-scoped `Total`. C3's command carries no selector at all and cites `88/88` cases against `70` discovered methods. `Total == discovery count` is unsatisfiable for any assembly containing `[Theory]` cases; C0 satisfied it only because `8/8` happened to have none. Options: (a) amend the contract with an explicit methods-vs-cases mapping and a named pre-existing-skip carve-out, then re-cite both rows; (b) re-run both under their own selectors and re-evidence; (c) reopen C3/C4 to `pending`. **RESOLVED 2026-07-27 by Administrator: amend the Checkpoint Execution Contract with an explicit test-methods-vs-`[Theory]`-cases mapping and a named pre-existing-skip carve-out, then re-cite C3 and C4 against the amended contract.**
- [x] [Review][Decision] [High] The `tests/tooling/production_deployment_evidence` lane has no closed create-baseline equation — story lines 999 and 1002 state it "has no create-story baseline (it entered the File List this phase), so no cumulative story delta is computable", but `git ls-tree 272c33bc tests/tooling/` lists the lane and both its modules exist at the baseline with `6 + 8 = 14` cases, matching the `14 -> 17` the 2026-07-26 chunk-3b row itself cites. Live discovery is `30`. Ledgered story deltas are `+10` and `+3` = `+13`, so `14 + 16 = 30` requires attributing an un-ledgered `+3`. This is the same false-escape class the Administrator already ruled on for `Cli.Tests`; the ruling was applied to one lane only. Options: attribute the `+3` to Story 27.3 (cumulative `+16`) or to a named external owner. Until attributed the lane has no closed equation, which is a `done` blocker. **RESOLVED 2026-07-27 by Administrator: attribute the un-ledgered `+3` to Story 27.3**, making the lane cumulative `+16` and the equation `14 create + 16 story + 0 external = 30`.
- [x] [Review][Decision] [Medium] `runtime_matches_reviewed_profile` can never be `true` for any runtime — the runtime profile's identity keys (`kube_context`, `kube_namespace`, `deployment_id`, `profile_id`, `evidence_root`, `declared_single_component_fault`) with capabilities `{lifecycle_writes, approval}` and `canonical_pg_onprem_profile()`'s (`profileId`, `kubeContext`, `kubeNamespace`, `postgresqlImage`, `componentType`, `maxConns`) with capabilities `{actorStateStore, strongReads, transactionRollback, ttl}` are structurally disjoint. ## Implementation Checkpoints

Story 27.3 is an explicitly approved C0/C1 qualification story. Every row is mandatory; completion qualifies only the immutable Production adapter profile and never closes A41.

**C2 added 2026-07-27 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-27 (DW 27.3-CR15).** C2 proves AC6 and is independent of C1: it is not gated on the five external C1 inputs, and passing it advances no C1 gate and does not enable Production lifecycle writes. AC5 continues to apply unchanged. The four `.tar.gz` archives the lane consumes are produced by `tools/publish-containers.ps1`, which the 2026-07-27 Epic 30 split assigns to **Story 30.3**; that story must not regress this lane. The forward dependency recorded earlier in this story stands with its owner updated from Story 30.1 to Story 30.3.

**C3 and C4 added 2026-07-27 by code review, per the Administrator decision of that date (option 1), executing the 2026-07-26 decision recorded in `Review Findings`.** That decision was that each of the three independently demonstrable lanes gains its own checkpoint row with individual owner, evidence command, review state and completion state; only the deployment-verification lane (C2) had received one, so the PG-v2 adapter/unit lane and the PG-ONPREM-1 manifest-guard lane were shipping, passing and closing findings on their own runners with nothing able to record it while every other row stayed gated behind the externally blocked C1. Both are marked complete on executed evidence from 2026-07-27. **Neither advances C1, enables Production lifecycle writes, unblocks Story 27.4, nor mutates A41**: they are unit and static-manifest lanes, and every C1 gate row remains `pending` / `not complete`.

**Ratified 2026-07-28 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-28**, executing the Administrator decision of 2026-07-27 (eighth-invocation review, option a): C3 and C4 are ratified through `correct-course` plus the matching `epics.md` amendment, matching the C2/AC6 precedent. Each now has a declaring acceptance criterion — **C3 proves AC7** and **C4 proves AC8** — added to both `epics.md` and this story's `## Acceptance Criteria` by that proposal. The sentence previously here, "No `epics.md` amendment is required, per the same decision", is superseded and removed: the 2026-07-27 review found that C3 and C4 had been created and marked `complete` with no approved sprint change and no `epics.md` entry, whereas the immediately preceding C2/AC6 carried both. Neither row's evidence, review state, completion state, nor completion date changes at this ratification; both keep their 2026-07-27 re-citation under the amended **Checkpoint Execution Contract** below.

### Checkpoint Execution Contract

Every C# checkpoint command below is a two-stage command contract: first build the named test project from the reviewed source with `DOTNET_CLI_USE_MSBUILD_SERVER=0 dotnet build <project> --configuration Release --disable-build-servers -m:1 /nr:false -p:NuGetAudit=false -p:MinVerVersionOverride=1.0.0`; then hash the reviewed source/profile and fresh assembly, discover the exact selected classes with `DiffEngine_Disabled=true dotnet exec <assembly> -list methods -noLogo`, and execute that same assembly with the table's selectors, `-parallel none -noLogo`. Immediately after execution, re-hash the source/profile and assembly and require byte-for-byte equality with the pre-execution values. The evidence must prove every selector discovers at least one unique method, that the executed **case** count is greater than zero and maps onto the selected **method** discovery count, and that `Errors`, `Failed`, and `Not Run` are all zero. A stale or concurrently changed source/profile/assembly, missing selector, zero discovery, unexplained count mismatch, or nonzero result fails the checkpoint even when the process exit code is zero.

**Amended 2026-07-27 by the Administrator during code review (eighth-invocation review).** The clause previously required `Total` to *equal* the selected discovery count and `Skipped` to be zero. Both were unsatisfiable in practice and the review found C3 and C4 marked `complete` on evidence the clause rejected. Two carve-outs are now explicit, and neither weakens the fail-closed intent:

- **Methods versus cases.** `dotnet exec -list methods` counts test *methods*; execution reports test *cases*, and one `[Theory]` method yields many. `Total == discovery count` therefore holds only for an assembly with no theories - true of C0 (`8/8`) by accident, false for any real lane. The requirement is now that `Total >= discovered methods > 0` and that the difference is attributable to `[Theory]` expansion; a `Total` **below** the discovered method count still fails, because that means methods did not run.
- **Pre-existing named skips.** A skip that is declared, owned, and unrelated to the checkpoint under proof does not fail it, provided the row names the skipped test and its owner. An undeclared or checkpoint-relevant skip still fails. `Not Run` remains strictly zero, since that indicates selection or execution failure rather than a declared exclusion.

Rows completed before this amendment must re-cite their evidence against it rather than inherit a pass.

The C1 Python checkpoint runs its exact `unittest` discovery command below with `PYTHONHASHSEED=0`, records the sorted fully qualified case IDs and their SHA-256, requires the listed inventory exactly once with zero failures/errors/skips, and then invokes the verifier against the same reviewed source/profile and immutable adapter evidence packet.

The verifier must record its expanded `kubectl`/Dapr/backend-evidence child commands, stdout, stderr, exit codes, UTC timestamps, pre/post workload identities, source/profile hashes, and immutable artifact hashes. **Corrected 2026-07-27 by code review (chunk 2):** a client-side `kube_context` name is not an identity control (per the 2026-07-26 decision at line 194) and is recorded only as the `kube_context_is_reviewed_label` observation, never a fail-closed gate; the binding identity is `KUBE_NAMESPACE` plus `PROFILE_ID`. A mocked control plane, missing expansion transcript, skipped fault/probe, or zero discovered observation still fails C1.

```bash
python3 tools/verify-access-telemetry-lifecycle.py --checkpoint adapter-profile --kube-context "$KUBE_CONTEXT" --namespace "$KUBE_NAMESPACE" --deployment-id "$DEPLOYMENT_ID" --profile-id "$PROFILE_ID" --workload-profile adr-27.1-two-writer-500eps --steady-state-minutes 30 --purge-backlog-records 150000 --evidence "$EVIDENCE_ROOT/27-3-adapter-profile-evidence.md"
```

#### C1 Gate Evidence Table

Every row below is an independent C1 gate and must pass on its own evidence. Per `epics.md` checkpoint-guard policy one row per gate is required, a gate cannot be marked complete because the C1 umbrella row is complete, and per AC5 any row not in `passed` completion state keeps Production writes disabled, Story 27.3 `in-progress`, Story 27.4 `backlog`, and A41 open. All rows begin `pending` review state and `not complete` completion state; no completion is claimed by adding or extending this table.

**Transition rule (added 2026-07-26 by code review, chunk 3b).** A row leaves `not complete` only when its named observation exists in the referenced evidence packet with `evidence_is_approval: true`, its accountable owner records the transition, and the code reviewer confirms the observation against the packet. `passed` is the only admissible completion state; `pending` review state and `passed` completion state cannot coexist. A row whose named producer cannot emit its observation stays `not complete` regardless of any other evidence.

**Capacity admission (added 2026-07-26 by code review, chunk 3b, per the Administrator decision).** The approved 2026-07-20 course correction's threshold table governs admission: steady state <=70% (300,647,710,720 bytes), reclamation peak <=80%, critical at 80% (343,597,383,680), Unhealthy at 90% (386,547,056,640). Where the narrative elsewhere in this story reads that a duration is admissible if it "fits the exact 400 GiB profile", the threshold table is the controlling gate and 100% occupancy is not admissible. A measured result at exactly 80% is treated as critical, not as an admissible peak. If the configured 24-hour target itself fails the 70% steady-state threshold, `PG-ONPREM-1` is rejected for that retention duration and a new correct-course decision is required; a passing 1-hour horizon licenses nothing on its own. Amending Task 1's own wording is a task-definition change and remains with the story owner.


**Producer correction 2026-07-27 by code review, per the Administrator decision of that date (option 2).** Thirteen rows (C1.1-C1.12 and C1.14) cited the shared `adapter-profile` command as their producer, while `tools/verify_access_telemetry_lifecycle.py` states it is "NOT a behavioural prober" and that "each of those rows names its own operator-executed command in the story's C1 Gate Evidence Table" - a cross-reference resolving in neither direction, because the table named no such command. Only the docstring half of the 2026-07-26 decision had been applied. Those thirteen rows are now marked **blocked** rather than given commands nobody can run: no operator-executable producer can exist for a CRUD, strong-read, ETag, transaction-fault, TTL, actor/Scheduler, request-bound, throughput, purge-backlog, isolation, encryption or reclamation observation while the Production lifecycle environment is disabled. Authoring thirteen unrunnable, unvalidatable commands would be exactly the paper evidence this story has refused throughout. **Owner:** Deployment adapter owner plus Hexalith Platform Operations, and the independent security reviewer for C1.11/C1.12. **Consequence:** these thirteen gates cannot be discharged by any artifact that exists today; they stay `pending` / `not complete`, so AC5 keeps Production writes disabled, Story 27.4 `backlog` and A41 open. **Reopen trigger:** the `PG-ONPREM-1` lifecycle Deployments are scaled above zero with the production flag and profile hash set and the clock authorities pointed at real endpoints; each row's own operator-executed command is then authored against the running target and recorded here before any completion state changes.

**Superseded 2026-07-28 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-28.** The paragraph above is append-only history and is not rewritten, but the thirteen rows it marked blocked are no longer Story 27.3's: **C1.1-C1.12 and C1.14 transferred to Story 27.5**, keeping their identifiers, and are removed from the table below. Their owners, consequence, and the reopen trigger recorded above carry over unchanged — that reopen trigger is now Story 27.5's activation gate, and authoring each row's operator-executed command is Story 27.5 scope. The rationale for the split is that `story-scope-guard.md:32-34` and `epics.md:555` permit this story to remain one tracking story only while **every** checkpoint carries an owner, an evidence command or artifact, a review state, and a completion state; thirteen rows with no producer removed that basis. The table below now carries twelve rows, every one naming a producer. Nothing about the transferred gates is weakened: AC5's fail-closed rule binds them under Story 27.5, so Production lifecycle writes stay disabled, Story 27.4 stays `backlog`, and A41 stays open until they pass.

Shared evidence packet for every row: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/tests/27-3-adapter-profile-evidence.md`, produced by the Production-Shaped Execution Contract command above against the immutable `PG-ONPREM-1` profile. Each row names the observation within that packet that proves it. Rows whose producer is a named test rather than the packet say so explicitly.

## Dev Notes

### Scope and Authority

- The exact Story/AC text comes from current `epics.md`; the accepted ADR and its **Production Adapter Qualification — PG-ONPREM-1** appendix are the technical authority. Story 27.2 implements portable runtime behavior; Story 27.3 qualifies the exact Production adapter and immutable deployment profile; Story 27.4 owns deployment-shaped lifecycle evidence, operations guidance, and the remaining A41 close-out. [Source: _bmad-output/planning-artifacts/epics.md#Story-27.3-Production-Adapter-and-Deployment-Profile; docs/dev/adr-27.1-001-access-telemetry-lifecycle.md#Production-Adapter-Qualification--PG-ONPREM-1]
- This story has one independently demonstrable outcome: an immutable, fully probed, configured-capacity-funded, backup/restore-documented, and separately approved `PG-ONPREM-1` C1 packet. It does not claim lifecycle close-out or mutate A41. **Narrowed 2026-07-28 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-28:** "fully probed" means the twelve retained C1 gate rows (C1.13 and C1.15-C1.25) plus the C2, C3 and C4 lanes; the thirteen capability gates that can only be observed against the running target (C1.1-C1.12 and C1.14) are Story 27.5's, and this story neither implements nor claims them. Every retained checkpoint names an owner, an evidence command or artifact, a review state, and a completion state, which is the `story-scope-guard.md:32-34` condition for remaining one tracking story.
- Run another correct-course decision if `PG-ONPREM-1` is rejected or discovery requires multiple adapters, a multi-node/replicated-storage platform, a general trusted-time/persistence/operations platform, a tenant-facing inspection product, or an independently deployable raw-console/OTLP privacy change. Keep writes disabled and A41 open.
- Bounded infrastructure telemetry remains separate from domain persistence. Do not add access telemetry to Hexalith.EventStore, repurpose domain state, or add direct infrastructure dependencies to product/lifecycle code.

### Architecture and Technology Guardrails

- Stable logical identities remain `memories-access-telemetry`, `AccessTelemetryLifecycleActor/global`, `memories-access-telemetry-clock`, `access-telemetry-store`, `access-telemetry-secrets`, and `access-telemetry-config` unless an accepted ADR amendment says otherwise.
- Repository pins control implementation: .NET SDK 10.0.302/net10.0/C# 14, Dapr .NET packages 1.18.4, Aspire AppHost SDK 13.4.6, StackExchange.Redis 3.0.17, xUnit v3 3.2.2, Shouldly 4.3.0, and NSubstitute 5.3.0. Do not upgrade dependencies in this story. The running Dapr runtime/component identity must be captured independently.
- Use current one-type-per-file, file-scoped namespace, XML documentation, source-generated logging, central package management, warnings-as-errors, `.slnx`, SDK containers, and existing composition roots.
- Preserve sanitization-before-enqueue, finite catalogs, bounded queues/batches/retries, signed independent UTC, source-age retention, transactional record/index writes, fixed-actor/reminder recovery, separate authorities, low-cardinality metrics, and business-readiness isolation.

### Latest Technical Information

Research snapshot: 2026-07-18. Repository pins remain authoritative.

- Dapr state-store feature tables are not behavioral certification. The official Dapr 1.18 Redis implementation states that Redis transactions lack rollback; the exact adapter must pass a partial-commit fault probe. [Source: https://docs.dapr.io/reference/components-reference/supported-state-stores/; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/dapr/components-contrib/v1.18.0/state/redis/redis.go]
- Dapr forwards `ttlInSeconds` to native stores but does not independently expire state; logical expiry and active purge remain authoritative and testable separately. [Source: https://docs.dapr.io/developing-applications/building-blocks/state-management/state-store-ttl/]
- Actor reminders survive activation failover through Dapr Scheduler; Production evidence must cover Scheduler/Placement persistence and replacement, not only the actor process. [Source: https://docs.dapr.io/developing-applications/building-blocks/actors/actors-timers-reminders/; https://docs.dapr.io/operations/hosting/kubernetes/kubernetes-persisting-scheduler/]
- Dapr metadata and health APIs expose runtime/component/actor/Scheduler identity needed in the evidence packet; do not infer runtime version from application packages. [Source: https://docs.dapr.io/reference/api/metadata_api/; https://docs.dapr.io/reference/api/health_api/]
- Actor state-store component changes require restart and component reload can transiently close/reinitialize the component; rollout/rollback must preserve manifests and prove epoch convergence. [Source: https://docs.dapr.io/operations/components/component-updates/; https://docs.dapr.io/reference/api/configuration_api/]
- Redis `everysec` AOF and `WAIT` do not establish a universal zero-loss guarantee. If Redis is selected, scope the tested fault/RPO precisely and separately prove dataset and AOF/disk reclamation for the test cohort. [Source: https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/persistence/; https://redis.io/docs/latest/commands/wait/; https://redis.io/docs/latest/commands/memory-usage/; https://redis.io/docs/latest/commands/info/]
- The current local integration environment reported Dapr runtime 1.18.1 while application Dapr packages are 1.18.4. This is baseline evidence of identity drift, not the Production identity; C1 must capture and pin the running Production runtime/component profile explicitly.

### Slice Proof

Story 27.3 is one approved qualification decision: prove or reject the single exact `PG-ONPREM-1` Production profile through C0/C1. **Re-derived 2026-07-26 by code review (chunk 3b):** the story remains one story under the `story-scope-guard.md` umbrella exemption **only conditionally**. The exemption's stated basis is that every checkpoint carries its own owner, evidence command/artifact, review state and completion state — C0, C1, C2, C3 and C4 in the checkpoint table, and each C1 gate in the gate evidence table. **That condition failed on 2026-07-27** (corrected by code review, eighth-invocation review): thirteen of the twenty-five C1 gate rows were recorded as **blocked** with no evidence command at all, so no basis remained for keeping them under one tracking story. **Restored 2026-07-28 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-28:** those thirteen rows — C1.1-C1.12 and C1.14 — transferred to Story 27.5, so the twelve C1 gate rows this story retains (C1.13 and C1.15-C1.25) each name a producer, as do C0, C1, C2, C3 and C4. The exemption holds again by observation rather than by assertion, and the check is repeatable: no row in the gate evidence table may read `Blocked` with no producer. Three lanes ship and prove independently of the externally-blocked C1 (the PostgreSQL-v2 adapter/unit lane, the `PG-ONPREM-1` manifest-guard lane, and the kind-based deployment-verification harness). **Reconciled 2026-07-27 by code review (chunk 3):** all three are now covered by named checkpoints — **the first two by checkpoints C3 and C4, not by C1 gate rows** (corrected 2026-07-27 by code review, eighth-invocation review: no C1 gate row names `DaprAccessTelemetryStateStoreTests`/`InMemoryAccessTelemetryStateStoreContractTests` or `ProductionDeploymentArtifactsTests`; only C1.13 names a test producer, and C1.11 explicitly disqualifies its unit test), and the third by checkpoint C2 (added by the approved 2026-07-27 Sprint Change Proposal, `DW 27.3-CR15`, now `resolved`). C2 remains `pending`/`not complete` because the CI run is red on the OpenBao runtime secret store (`DW 27.3-CR17`), so the lane is not yet claimable as *passing* Story 27.3 evidence — but it is no longer missing an acceptance criterion or a checkpoint. **Updated 2026-07-28:** C3 and C4 are likewise no longer missing an acceptance criterion — the approved 2026-07-28 correction ratifies both and declares them through the new AC7 and AC8, so all three independently shipping lanes now have both a criterion and a checkpoint. A passing outcome requires every immutable image, capability, bounded fault, configured-retention capacity, backup/restore, security, and reclamation result plus separate Platform Operations and security approvals. Rejection is also bounded, but it keeps writes disabled and requires another course correction.

The split boundary is strict: 27.1 owns the accepted portable design, 27.2 owns portable runtime behavior, 27.3 owns exact-adapter qualification and the immutable deployment profile, 27.5 owns the thirteen capability gates that require the running target (added 2026-07-28 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-28), 27.4 owns C2-C6/runbooks/reconciliation/A41 close-out, and Epic 28 owns EventStore runtime adoption. Any general platform, tenant-facing product, multiple-adapter rollout, or missing runtime feature returns to planning.

### Implementation Plan

- Add a standard-library-only C1 adapter-profile contract with explicit Production identity validation, ADR workload constants, checked capacity arithmetic, and immutable profile/mutation hashes.
- Keep the deployment collector read-only and fail closed: collect redacted Kubernetes structure, reject disabled lifecycle writers or unproven `state.redis` capability, and emit a non-approval evidence packet without enabling Production writes.
- Provision and qualify only the exact `PG-ONPREM-1` target recorded in ADR 27.1; pin every live image by digest, including PostgreSQL and new lifecycle/clock CI digests, and reject profile drift.
- Replace the unsupported Dapr Query path with deterministic transactional expiry buckets before exercising PostgreSQL v2.
- Require zero skipped C1 probes, zero acknowledged loss for the declared pod/process fault, configured-retention capacity, backup/restore evidence, explicit non-HA limits, and separate hash-bound Platform Operations and security approvals before changing this story from `in-progress`.

#### Accepted blocker - AC4 independent security approver (recorded 2026-07-26, code review chunk 3b)

- **Blocker:** AC4 requires Hexalith Platform Operations approval and a *separate* security reviewer approval of identity, secrets, TLS, network, authorization, encryption, privacy and evidence integrity. No independent security approver is assigned. The only assignment on record named the Platform Operations owner plus a persona acting for that same human, which does not satisfy the separation requirement and has been withdrawn.
- **Owner:** Product Owner, to nominate an independent security approver; Hexalith Platform Operations remains the AC4 operations approver.
- **Consequence:** C1 gates C1.23 (Platform Operations approval), C1.24 (non-HA acknowledgement) and C1.25 (security reviewer approval) cannot leave `pending`/`not complete`. C1 therefore cannot pass, Production lifecycle writes stay disabled, Story 27.3 stays `in-progress`, Story 27.4 stays `backlog`, and A41 stays open. This blocker is independent of, and additional to, the five external C1 inputs and the `.example.invalid` clock authorities.
- **Reopen trigger:** a named human other than the Platform Operations owner is recorded as the accountable security reviewer for Story 27.3, and records their AC4 approval against the immutable evidence packet.
- **Not a substitute:** no persona, agent, workflow, or self-review may be recorded as the independent security approver.

- 2026-07-26 (third dev-story invocation): Applied all 38 open chunk-3a `[Review][Patch]` items and the patch work behind the 7 chunk-3a `[Review][Decision]` items, then re-derived C1 as still rejected. Task 1 remains unchecked and Story 27.3 remains `in-progress`; no Production enablement, live deployment mutation (`kubectl apply` was not run), approval claim, Story 27.4 artifact, or A41 mutation occurred. The 3 `[Review][Action]` items stay open: they are `correct-course` activities (`DW 27.3-CR14`/`CR15`/`CR16`), not dev-story scope.
  - **The one genuine product defect: `StaleIndex` orphaned the record it was protecting.** `DeleteAndVerifyAsync` decided bucket membership on `RecordId` alone, so a superseded or foreign expiry entry sharing `(ExpiryMinute, Shard)` with the live record pruned the *live* entry before returning `StaleIndex`. `GetDueEntriesAsync` reads purge candidates only from buckets, so that record could never be rediscovered — permanently unpurgeable, while the caller saw a clean denial. The fix matches the complete five-member entry identity in the membership check, in `CreateBucketRemoval`, in `RemoveBucketEntryAsync`, and in the post-delete verification re-read, and is mirrored in `InMemoryAccessTelemetryStateStore`, whose entry key now carries the full identity. Driven by a genuine RED: both tests that asserted the orphaning as correct failed on the fix; both now assert the opposite and additionally require the record to still be returned by `GetDueEntriesAsync`.
  - **The in-memory adapter no longer diverges from the adapter it stands in for.** All four behaviours the portable lifecycle checkpoints can observe are aligned: a duplicate entry slot returns `Conflict` decided before any mutation (it used to throw `ArgumentException` *after* `_records` was written — a non-atomic write); `VerificationFailed` is reachable through a `SuppressRecordDeletion` hook plus the same strong post-delete verification; `ttlInSeconds` is validated and observable instead of silently discarded; and the due scan is minute-major over an active-minute catalog that prunes drained minutes and leaves untraversed minutes alone. Seven new cases pin each behaviour. `LastTransactionOperationCount` is now derived from the committed operation set (3 opening a new minute, 2 otherwise — exactly the Dapr adapter's counts) and read under the lock, replacing a `ShouldBe(2)` tautology in two checkpoints.
  - **Guards that could not fail are now bound.** The three deployment-guard helpers were structurally unsound in five distinct ways; each is fixed and mutation-proven, including the one that mattered most — pg_hba is first-match-wins, so a bare `host … scram-sha-256` line above the reject rules accepted plaintext TCP while all four presence assertions passed. The ordering-parity tests never exercised the `Shard` or `RecordId` tiebreakers they claimed to pin; two fixtures now do, and removing either tiebreaker fails exactly one of them. The `TransactionalDaprState` fake now enforces the partition/TTL metadata contract and strong-only reads, so an adapter that stopped sending either no longer stays green.
  - **A manifest defect the connection-pool guard was blind to.** The guard hardcoded two writers while the shipped overlay is `replicas: 0` with no `strategy:` block, so Kubernetes' default 25% surge would have added a third sidecar pool (3 x 40 + 13 = 133 against `max_connections=100`) mid-probe. The Deployment now pins `maxSurge: 0`/`maxUnavailable: 1`, and the guard derives peak pods from the rendered overlay instead of a constant.
  - **The C1 verifier now has real producers and an honest packet.** `_run_dapr_version` and `calculate_capacity` were defined and called from no evidence path, so AC1's runtime identity was never captured and gate C1.13 had no producer at all; both are wired. Capacity arithmetic gained the missing `schedulerBytes` and host-filesystem-headroom operands, the `max(137438953472, ceil(base/4))` reclamation floor, a pinned durability multiplier, and admission against the approved 70/80/90% table. `_normalize_bytes` now rejects every unit-less value, so `"400"` meaning 400 GB can no longer read as 400 bytes. Command failures become recorded observations instead of tracebacks that leave no packet. The packet carries per-command timestamps, reviewed-source hashes, pre/post workload identity, and the seven AC3/AC4 attestation fields — all defaulting to `unrecorded`, never to a synthesized claim.
- 2026-07-26 (third dev-story invocation): Remediation runtime defect checklist, applicability re-derived from this session's actual diff. Changed surfaces are the two `IAccessTelemetryStateStore` implementations, their test doubles and fixtures, the deployment-guard tests, one Dapr component-adjacent Deployment field (`strategy.rollingUpdate`), the C1 verifier tooling, the production-deployment PowerShell lane, and CI wiring. **Category 1 (Dapr workflow activity registration): not applicable** — no workflow, child workflow, activity, or DI registration changed. **Category 2 (observed child workflows): not applicable** — no orchestration changed. **Category 3 (owner-checked cleanup and dedup): applicable, and it is where this session's one real defect lived.** The review-found `StaleIndex` index-orphaning defect is recorded here as a Category 3 item per the policy's review gate: a cleanup path that mutated shared state on a match that was not the entry it was authorised to remove. Backed by executed passing evidence in the `85/85` AccessTelemetry.Tests run — owner-checked cleanup by `DeleteAndVerifyAsync_IndexEntryRefersToSupersededEnvelope_ReturnsStaleIndexAndProtectsLiveRecord` and `DeleteAndVerifyAsync_SupersededEntrySharingTheLiveSlot_LeavesTheLiveEntryPurgeable` (both assert the live entry survives *and* the record stays purgeable); duplicate-detector false positives by `WriteRecordAndIndexAsync_TtlReapedRecordWithLingeringBucketEntry_ReturnsConflictWithoutResurrection` and `WriteRecordAndIndexAsync_EntrySlotAlreadyOccupied_ReturnsConflictAndCommitsNothing`; owner-check races by `WriteRecordAndIndexAsync_ConcurrentCatalogWriteBetweenReadAndCommit_ThrowsAndCommitsNoPartialState`, `DeleteAndVerifyAsync_BackendKeepsRecordAfterAcknowledgedDelete_ReturnsVerificationFailed` and `DeleteAndVerifyAsync_BackendKeepsTheBucketEntryAfterAcknowledgedRewrite_ReturnsVerificationFailed`; and index repair under late events by `DeleteAndVerifyAsync_TtlReapedRecordWithLingeringBucketEntry_ReturnsAlreadyAbsentAndPrunesEntry` and `GetDueEntriesAsync_AfterAMinuteIsFullyPurged_PrunesThatMinuteOnTheNextScan`. Migration inventory miscounting and tenant index repair remain untouched: this is a single global infrastructure store keyed by `RecordId` under one fixed actor, not tenant-partitioned state. **Correction to the previous phase's record, per the 2026-07-26 Administrator decision:** the `project-context.md` tenant-isolation rule is **no longer cited as satisfied for this lane**. `DeleteAndVerifyAsync_EntryCarryingAnotherTenantMarker_IsDeniedAndLeavesTheRecordPurgeable` proves envelope-binding denial, not physical cross-tenant isolation — the record key carries no tenant dimension, so both of its records resolve to one state key by construction.   - **Connection-pool exhaustion, fixed.** `maxConns: 64` on `access-telemetry-store` against two ADR writers demanded 128 backend connections while `access-telemetry-postgresql.yaml` starts PostgreSQL with `max_connections=100`. The 500 events/s probe would have failed on connection exhaustion and been misread as a capacity limit of the profile. `maxConns` is now `40`: `2 x 40 + 3 superuser-reserved + 10 evidence sessions = 93 <= 100`. Driven by a genuine RED — the new `ProductionOverlay_AccessTelemetryConnectionPoolFitsPostgreSqlMaxConnections` failed at `141 > 100`, then passed after the fix. The guard parses both operands out of the rendered overlay rather than matching strings, and refuses to run its arithmetic if `reserved_connections=` is ever overridden, so the derivation cannot go stale silently.
  - **Profile security contracts, now bound.** AC4 requires an independent security reviewer to approve identity, secrets, TLS, authorization, and encryption for the exact profile, but six of those properties had no static guard and could drift green: `access-telemetry-secrets` `skipVerify`/`tlsServerName`, the store's `actorStateStore: "true"`, pg_hba `hostssl … scram-sha-256` + `hostnossl … reject`, the init-SQL least-privilege grants/revokes, both RBAC secret-reader Roles, and the deny-default lifecycle ACL. `ProductionOverlay_AccessTelemetryProfileSecurityContractsAreBound` binds all of them structurally — exact `resourceNames`/`resources`/`verbs` sequences and exact `allowedSecrets` membership, not substring presence.
  - **The new guard was mutation-proven, not RED-proven.** It passed on first authoring, which proves nothing about drift detection, so each binding was verified by injecting the drift it exists to catch: `skipVerify:"true"`, `actorStateStore:"false"`, a dropped `hostnossl … reject` line, RBAC verbs widened to `get,list`, ACL `defaultAction: allow`, and an extra secret smuggled into `allowedSecrets`. Every injection failed the suite (the RBAC widening tripped two tests), and the baseline returned to `9/0/0` after each revert.

Only the 8 paths named above joined this phase; the correct total is 45, not 47. This is a two-path overcount that the later `matched 47/47` rows (the following `2026-07-26 | dev-story` row and the `2026-07-27 | dev-story` row) and this section's own subsequent "47 -> 48" heading carry forward uncorrected — each should be read as one-lower (46/46, then 46 -> 47) than stated. This does **not** change the current, independently-verified File List: both `## File Scope` and this `File List` currently enumerate exactly 48 unique paths each, confirmed identical as sets by this and the preceding chunk's review — the overcount is confined to the intermediate narrative arithmetic, not the present end-state. Reopen trigger: none: this note is the reconciliation the phase ledger requires; no further repair is needed unless a future count reintroduces the same error.
